The dimensionless equation is `u_t = kappa*u_xx` on `0<x<1` with `u(0,t)=u(1,t)=0`. The initial field belongs to the first 32 sine modes. The diffusivity lies in `[0.5,1.5]`. You receive noisy, possibly missing observations at two positive times; the retained coordinates are exact. Each observation has independent zero-mean Gaussian noise with the same known absolute standard deviation `sigma` for that case. There are at least 64 observations at each time. All parameters needed to interpret the observations are public.\n\nYour entry point must accept:\n\n```text\npython solve.py --input /input --output /output --seed 0\n```\n\n`manifest.json` in the input directory lists `cases`, each with `id`, `path` and difficulty `level`. Each case directory contains `observations.csv` with columns `x,t,y`, `metadata.json` with `kappa`, `sigma`, observation `times` and `future_time`, and `target_grid.npy`. The target grid is `x_j=j/257` for `j=1,...,256`. Write exactly `cases/<id>/u0.npy` for every case: finite little-endian float64, shape `(256,)`. Do not write reward files or extra output files. A submitted program must solve all instances from their provided inputs; `--seed` controls your algorithm and does not identify a generated system. Object arrays, pickle, complex numbers, NaN/Infinity, links and paths outside the output directory are forbidden.\n\nThe evaluator constructs the full 256-mode sine interpolant of your output: `b_k=(2/257)*sum_j u0_j*sin(pi*k*j/257)`, for `k=1,...,256`. Its propagation to coordinate `x` and time `t` is `sum_k b_k*exp(-kappa*pi^2*k^2*t)*sin(pi*k*x)`. It does not truncate your output to 32 modes. The hidden initial-field relative Euclidean error must be at most `0.15`; the propagated-field relative error at the public `future_time` and target grid must be at most `0.03`. Every relative-error denominator is `max(norm(truth),1e-12)`. The per-case continuous score is `100*(0.8*clip(1-E_init/0.30,0,1)+0.2*clip(1-E_forward/0.06,0,1))`. All 12 hidden cases, including four per difficulty, must pass in each of three clean replays for the task reward to be 1. Illegal/missing outputs and worker failures score 0. High scores or low observation residual alone do not establish correct recovery.\n\nYou may use the common public evaluation interface on all six development cases. It returns `R=||H_obs(u0)-y||^2/(m*sigma^2)` at the actual observation locations and times, plus roughness `sqrt(mean((D2(u0)/h^2)^2))`, with `h=1/257` and zero boundary padding. The dimensionless equation is `u_t = kappa*u_xx` on `0<x<1` with `u(0,t)=u(1,t)=0`. The initial field belongs to the first 32 sine modes. The diffusivity lies in `[0.5,1.5]`. You receive noisy, possibly missing observations at two positive times; the retained coordinates are exact. Each observation has independent zero-mean Gaussian noise with the same known absolute standard deviation `sigma` for that case. There are at least 64 observations at each time. All parameters needed to interpret the observations are public.\n\nYour entry point must accept:\n\n```text\npython solve.py --input /input --output /output --seed 0\n```\n\n`manifest.json` in the input directory lists `cases`, each with `id`, `path` and difficulty `level`. Each case directory contains `observations.csv` with columns `x,t,y`, `metadata.json` with `kappa`, `sigma`, observation `times` and `future_time`, and `target_grid.npy`. The target grid is `x_j=j/257` for `j=1,...,256`. Write exactly `cases/<id>/u0.npy` for every case: finite little-endian float64, shape `(256,)`. Do not write reward files or extra output files. A submitted program must solve all instances from their provided inputs; `--seed` controls your algorithm and does not identify a generated system. Object arrays, pickle, complex numbers, NaN/Infinity, links and paths outside the output directory are forbidden.\n\nThe evaluator constructs the full 256-mode sine interpolant of your output: `b_k=(2/257)*sum_j u0_j*sin(pi*k*j/257)`, for `k=1,...,256`. Its propagation to coordinate `x` and time `t` is `sum_k b_k*exp(-kappa*pi^2*k^2*t)*sin(pi*k*x)`. It does not truncate your output to 32 modes. The hidden initial-field relative Euclidean error must be at most `0.15`; the propagated-field relative error at the public `future_time` and target grid must be at most `0.03`. Every relative-error denominator is `max(norm(truth),1e-12)`. The per-case continuous score is `100*(0.8*clip(1-E_init/0.30,0,1)+0.2*clip(1-E_forward/0.06,0,1))`. All 12 hidden cases, including four per difficulty, must pass in each of three clean replays for the task reward to be 1. Illegal/missing outputs and worker failures score 0. High scores or low observation residual alone do not establish correct recovery.\n\nYou may use the common public evaluation interface on all six development cases. It returns `R=||H_obs(u0)-y||^2/(m*sigma^2)` at the actual observation locations and times, plus roughness `sqrt(mean((D2(u0)/h^2)^2))`, with `h=1/257` and zero boundary padding. out.append((ei, ef))\n    return np.array(out)\n\nfor p_true in [1.75, 2.0, 2.25]:\n    print('=== ptrue', p_true)\n    for pest in [2.0, 2.25]:\n        for method in
